# BRD v1.1 requirements traceability — R11 and development slices 0a-1f, 2a-2j, 3a-3k, 4a-4f, 5a-5i, 6a-6i, 6k, 6j, 6l, 6m, 6n, 6o, 6p, 6q, 6r, 6s, 6s-2, 6t, 6u, 6v, 6w, 6x, 6y, 6z, 6aa, 6ab, 6ac, 6ad, 7a-7d, 7e, 7f, 7g, 0b-2, 0b-3, 0b-4, 9a, 9b, 10h, 10e, 10c, 10d, 10f, 10g, 10i, 10j, 10b, 10a

All **176 requirements** are assessed: 148 functional and 28 non-functional. Source: Health-AI-Orchestration-Platform-BRD-v1.1.docx, SHA-256 `8dcc0a392fe46e342d8524a722328e2e8889143d532db9769d09683caf30a1c8`. The exact requirement and acceptance text is preserved in [the source extract](requirements/brd-v1.1.json) and [the complete CSV](requirements/traceability.csv).

| Implementation assessment | Count |
| --- | ---: |
| implemented-local | 15 |
| partial | 150 |
| not-built | 2 |
| unverified | 9 |

**Formal acceptance: 0 accepted; 176 pending.** Local implementation and synthetic assertions are not clinical, field, regulatory, integration or production acceptance. These counts are not a completion percentage. BRD R1/R2/R3 are scope phases; engineering R10–R16 are delivery batches and are not equivalent. Targets are planned primary work batches, subject to dependencies and findings.

Evidence paths identify related code/tests or explicit gap documentation, not full test coverage. All requirements retain their source acceptance criterion in the CSV/JSON. The five future batches, open R11 dependencies and other remaining work are in [REMAINING-RELEASES.md](REMAINING-RELEASES.md).

## BR-UX

| BR-UX-01 — One task entry point | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | One authenticated patient workspace with programme and agent panels. Independent image-provider flow is available in the synthetic visit workspace and Connections screen. App-first slice: authenticated /app reuses the care workspace, with Today, People, Referrals, Sync and More; a browser phone preview is tested with synthetic data. Android Browser Helper source opens the same app, but APK/device verification remains pending. Guided ASHA Home adds three explicit English/draft-Hindi navigation choices and a follow-on record prompt; actual phone geometry, audited selection, unsaved Home and Back safeguards pass locally. Full step-by-step capture and representative worker training/usability remain pending. D02 isolated /today candidate reuses server scope and durable audit with a read-only identity-context projection; see docs/current/d02/SPECIFICATION.md and VALIDATION.md. Formal and representative-worker acceptance remain pending. D03 isolated routine candidate reuses current guided controls and four audited commands bound to one selected person;50 built synthetic backend/browser checks cover shared three-pack capture, provenance, lost committed acknowledgement, explicit conflict recovery and changed-authority refusal. Existing encrypted local component full-fixture p95 is separately measured under the unchanged500ms target; the connected screen does not acknowledge durable local work. Final CI/review/host gate and representative-device/worker/formal acceptance remain pending. See docs/current/d03/SPECIFICATION.md and EVIDENCE.json. Overnight recovery checkpoint (11 October 2026): originating organisation and unresolved exact-key continuity are repaired; a bounded audited higher-version/no-receipt disposition permits explicit stale recovery. Current source/evidence and the unresolved full-unit contention failure are in docs/current/d03/OVERNIGHT-RECOVERY-20261011.md. Local browser/component evidence is not full NFR, CI, hosted or formal acceptance. Overnight access/recovery checkpoint (11 October 2026): final consistent Routine/Today access checks, bounded ACCESS append liveness, original-organisation dispatch and original-account exact-attempt recovery have scoped native/browser and final203-unit/API/restore evidence. See docs/current/d03/OVERNIGHT-AUTHORITY-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUTHORITY.json. Current-head CI, complete applicable physical/reference-device NFR and hosted A10 remain pending; wider captured-member helper and formal acceptance are not closed. Integrated audit qualification (11 October 2026): current-membership append/postcheck and native excess-count witness pass fresh current305 full205/10791 guarded205; same-runtime canonical166/14529 passes guarded166 with one inherited media skip and excluded old13 unit fixture declared. All380 prior guard records remain exact. See docs/current/d03/AUDIT-GUARD-QUALIFICATION-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUDIT-GUARD.json for exact source/setup limits and saved Git receipts. Complete real UI/reference NFR02, originalNFR5, final-head CI, separate final review and A10 remain pending; physical/clinical/formal acceptance is unchanged. Current NFR checkpoint (11 October 2026): authorization63/0, classifier11/0 and native source-drift10/0 pass; load12/3 fails with360/400 slots complete under unchanged minima/budgets. | BR-UX-03 — Voice text and guided entry | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Structured/text capture and gated on-device speech transcript review. Slices 2b and 3a: attached voice notes play back in the visit and can be deleted from drafts or discarded from the device queue. Slice 3d: voice notes recorded in the field notebook (24 kbps, up to 60 s) and queued offline. Slice 12a: a microphone on every text and number field of the workspace and the field notebook; one tap listens once, what was heard is reviewed and inserted only on request, and nothing is saved by speaking. | BR-UX-07 — Resume interrupted work | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Server drafts, local encrypted drafts and command replay support restart recovery. App-first A02 adds a public locked offline /app shell sharing the unchanged encrypted field vault: current prepared visits, capture and sync; account/packet-owner/lifetime gates, ciphertext-only storage and public-only caches; weight and notes survive actual offline browser reload. Synced visits remain unconfirmed until online review. The app view does not claim a full offline caseload or approved-guidance cache. Independent A02 review fixed actual withdrawn-assignment disclosure: connected unlock and reconnect recheck every current prepared patient in its original organisation; positive refusal/login redirect locks and redacts, while unreachable transport retains only bounded signed offline access. Prepared and registration copy recovery now retains originating-account encrypted proof and exact observed receipt variants without blind clinical resend. Shared field hooks preserve latest-book receipt/tombstone state under queued writes and account transitions. | Physical-device crash, incoming-call, eviction and network-loss acceptance; broader offline caseload and native Android qualification remain. | | BR-ID-01 — Workforce lifecycle | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Account-bound invitations, role/team changes, suspension and immediate online access checks. Slice 10d adds workforce import from a CSV file: an owner uploads a roster (name, account code, role, team, status, optional external staff id) for a dry run that lists invitations to create, memberships to update or suspend, rows rejected with reasons and conflicts under a plan hash, downloads a rejection report and applies exactly the previewed plan (refused if anything changed since), on the slice 1d rules, in batches of at most 100 rows with an audit record. | Formal verification, scheduled or API workforce feeds, upstream de-provisioning, batches over 100 rows, verified and displayed external staff ids, and production identity governance. | [lib/saha/continuity.ts](lib/saha/continuity.ts)<br>[lib/saha/platform.ts](lib/saha/platform.ts)<br>[tests/continuity-smoke.mjs](tests/continuity-smoke.mjs)<br>[CORE-CONTINUITY.md](CORE-CONTINUITY.md)<br>[lib/saha/roster.ts](lib/saha/roster.ts)<br>[tests/roster-contract.mjs](tests/roster-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/roster-smoke.mjs](tests/roster-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/workforce-import.ts](lib/saha/workforce-import.ts)<br>[lib/saha/workforce-import-server.ts](lib/saha/workforce-import-server.ts)<br>[tests/workforce-import-contract.mjs](tests/workforce-import-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/workforce-import-smoke.mjs](tests/workforce-import-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-10D-WORKFORCE-IMPORT.md](docs/current/SLICE-10D-WORKFORCE-IMPORT.md) |
| BR-ID-02 — Scoped permissions | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Organisation, team, role and direct-worker scope enforced on reads/writes/exports. Slice 1e: time-limited, revocable delegation of team supervision (assignment, rights, action-plan and referral stewardship) and of reporting-only aggregates, enforced on the server, ineffective once either party stops qualifying, each use audited. Slice 6p (independent review): the delegation limit counts only delegations still in force, so ended and revoked records no longer block grants; the audit-chain export and data exit give the sandbox provider role no patient scope. Slice 6q: stored delegation records are capped at 1,000 (kept as evidence); export retries re-check care scope; shadow runs are limited to the runner's care scope. Slices R08 and R09 added owner-managed facility/locality scope and programme grants; slice 13a applies both in every normallyAllowed check and in the SQL pre-filter (viewer-scoped reads, patient list and detail, work lists, identifier search), and refuses API requests another website started. Slice 13f: the cross-site gate no longer depends on how the path is spelled (an encoded /api/ path was let through), and receiving workers and assignees must be inside the facility, locality and programme policy. D02 isolated /today candidate reuses server scope and durable audit with a read-only identity-context projection; see docs/current/d02/SPECIFICATION.md and VALIDATION.md. Formal and representative-worker acceptance remain pending. Overnight access/recovery checkpoint (11 October 2026): final consistent Routine/Today access checks, bounded ACCESS append liveness, original-organisation dispatch and original-account exact-attempt recovery have scoped native/browser and final203-unit/API/restore evidence. See docs/current/d03/OVERNIGHT-AUTHORITY-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUTHORITY.json. Current-head CI, complete applicable physical/reference-device NFR and hosted A10 remain pending; wider captured-member helper and formal acceptance are not closed. Integrated audit qualification (11 October 2026): current-membership append/postcheck and native excess-count witness pass fresh current305 full205/10791 guarded205; same-runtime canonical166/14529 passes guarded166 with one inherited media skip and excluded old13 unit fixture declared. All380 prior guard records remain exact. See docs/current/d03/AUDIT-GUARD-QUALIFICATION-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUDIT-GUARD.json for exact source/setup limits and saved Git receipts. Complete real UI/reference NFR02, originalNFR5, final-head CI, separate final review and A10 remain pending; physical/clinical/formal acceptance is unchanged. Current NFR checkpoint (11 October 2026): authorization63/0, classifier11/0 and native source-drift10/0 pass; load12/3 fails with360/400 slots complete under unchanged minima/budgets. Fixed1000 realUI control finishes8000 records, Routine p953132.309ms exceeds3s; desktop surrogate is not physical/R1 acceptance. Navigation proposal failed22/1 and is withheld, original Today unchanged. See docs/current/d03/NFR-CHECKPOINT-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-NFR-CHECKPOINT.json. Current-head CI/A9/A10 and formal criteria remain open; no implementation status is promoted. Only the existing DELETE adds the same trusted actor active-membership predicate; separate code review, fresh build and all nine native HTTP scenarios/64 named check groups pass with independent whole-row/source/build evidence verification; normal CI fixture qualification, exact successor CI/full NFR and hosted acceptance remain pending. See docs/current/d03/TELEMETRY-RETENTION-QUALIFICATION-20261011.md. | BR-ID-04 — Caseload assignment and transfer | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | R10 transfers open patient work atomically, records effective time and receiving-worker acknowledgement. Slice 1b adds bulk caseload transfer with scheduled effective dates, re-checks at application, bulk acknowledgement and a responsibility-gap view with urgent work first. Slice 1f applies due scheduled transfers automatically on the first read or command at or after their effective time, with the same re-checks under the scheduling steward's authority, exactly once under the organisation-wide commit guard, a revision and audit event per application and a held state with the reason when a re-check fails; and adds whole-household transfer, refused when any current member is outside the requester's scope. Slice 10a: a Cloudflare Cron Trigger (every five minutes, set in the hosted staging configuration only) runs a scheduled job that applies due scheduled transfers through the same all-or-nothing path (slice 6ae), at most 10 per organisation per run, oldest effective time first, with the scheduling steward's authority re-checked, a revision and audit event per application naming a system actor, and a refused transfer held whole and never retried by the job; where no trigger is configured, application time still depends on use. Verified in contract tests and a local scheduled-handler check, not on Cloudflare. | A timed trigger that has actually run on the hosted Worker (the job needs the Workers Paid plan and has not been seen firing on Cloudflare; on a host with no trigger the application time depends on use), locality transfer, field acceptance of handovers and approved urgency rules. | [lib/saha/continuity.ts](lib/saha/continuity.ts)<br>[lib/saha/platform.ts](lib/saha/platform.ts)<br>[tests/continuity-smoke.mjs](tests/continuity-smoke.mjs)<br>[CORE-CONTINUITY.md](CORE-CONTINUITY.md)<br>[lib/saha/caseload.ts](lib/saha/caseload.ts)<br>[lib/saha/caseload-schedule.ts](lib/saha/caseload-schedule.ts)<br>[tests/caseload-smoke.mjs](tests/caseload-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/caseload-contract.mjs](tests/caseload-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/caseload-schedule-smoke.mjs](tests/caseload-schedule-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/caseload-schedule-contract.mjs](tests/caseload-schedule-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-1B-CASELOAD.md](docs/current/SLICE-1B-CASELOAD.md)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-1F-EFFECTIVE-TRANSFERS.md](docs/current/SLICE-1F-EFFECTIVE-TRANSFERS.md)<br>[lib/saha/scheduled-job.ts](lib/saha/scheduled-job.ts)<br>[lib/saha/scheduled-review.ts](lib/saha/scheduled-review.ts)<br>[worker/index.ts](worker/index.ts)<br>[tests/scheduled-job-contract.mjs](tests/scheduled-job-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/scheduled-local-check.mjs](tests/scheduled-local-check.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-10A-SCHEDULED-TRIGGER.md](docs/current/SLICE-10A-SCHEDULED-TRIGGER.md)<br>[scripts/cloudflare-staging.mjs](scripts/cloudflare-staging.mjs)<br>[tests/cloudflare-staging-contract.mjs](tests/cloudflare-staging-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/HOSTED-STAGING.md](docs/current/HOSTED-STAGING.md) |
| BR-ID-05 — Patient matching with human resolution | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | R10 scoped exact/similar-name candidates, explicit separate/provisional decisions and clinician resolution. Slice 1c: merges need two different stewards and are never automatic; merged records are not offered as matches. Slice 10e: search by one complete issuer identifier (a linked ABHA number, RCH ID or Ni-kshay ID) inside the searcher's own care scope only; exact whole-value formats, so a partial or wildcard identifier is refused rather than searched; each search is a material read audited before it returns and a search that finds nothing is audited too; per-searcher limits (30 an hour, 8 finding nothing an hour, 24 a day); a record outside the scope gets the same answer as no record. The programme issuer formats are illustrative and unchecked against the registries. Atomic-review slice: D1 admission reservations bound concurrent ABHA review probes before lookup; stable retries do not probe again, generic refusal is preserved, and 32 local concurrent attempts admit exactly three. | BR-ID-08 — Correctable identity links | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | Identity revisions, EHR link revocation and changed-link rejection retain history. Slice 1c: two-steward link-based merge that preserves original records and receipts, invalidates pending approvals, queues downstream corrections and supports unmerge with review of work created while merged. Slice 6r: a clinician holding only emergency access to a record cannot propose, decide, unmerge or resolve a merge naming it. Slice 6u: the merge view returns the case reason and evidence only to a viewer who may see both records. | BR-DATA-02 — Source and timing for every fact | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | "Visits record source/time; programme answers retain author and explicit absence values. Slice 2c: each measurement may carry its own source (measured or reported) and observation time, or a separate missing reason (not asked, declined, unknown, not applicable), checked on every save path and carried into FHIR observation times, methods and narratives; older visits fall back to the visit-level values. Slice 2e: each measurement records who recorded it (account and role from the server identity, never from the request; older facts show ""not recorded"") and a verification state (unverified, verified or disputed by a clinician with time and reason), changed only by an appended, audited, scope-checked clinician review on a confirmed visit, shown on the visit screen and carried into FHIR notes and performers. Slice 2g: the visit notes and each programme answer also record who recorded them (server identity and role at save time; older entries ""not recorded"") and a clinician verification state changed only by an appended, audited, scope-checked review on a confirmed visit; an owner can require supervisor sign-off before programme submissions are sent, recorded by a supervisor of the patient's team or a supervision delegate, bound to the visit's facts and answers so any later change makes it stale, and checked on the server before executePlan and again inside the delivery checkpoint; shown on the visit screen and the submissions view. Slice 6z: on a visit with no recorded entry or measurement author (recorded before slices 2e and 2g), the confirmer and, for a programme answer, the person who saved the answers are treated as authors, so they cannot verify their own entries; the person who saved the programme answers cannot sign the visit off. Slice 10g: a care-team member in normal scope (an ASHA only for an assigned patient) can raise a dispute on one confirmed measurement, the visit notes or one programme answer, with a reason and a reference; while it is open on a current visit, no programme plan or EHR publication that would carry the item is prepared, approved or run, the reason is shown, and the same check is repeated on a fresh read inside the delivery and publication checkpoint so a dispute raised between the checkpoint and the request stops the request and closes the item as not sent; a clinician in scope resolves it by appending evidence (closing it as standing needs a clinician who neither recorded the item nor raised the dispute), and the dispute and the original value stay on the record; a correction draft carries still-open disputes forward. An owner can set the sign-off requirement per programme and per destination (versioned with the organisation default, audited; a destination rule wins over programme rules, which win over the default, and the default covers programme destinations only), and a supervisor of the patient's team can withdraw a current sign-off with a reason, which holds sending until a supervisor signs the visit off again whatever the rules say. Nothing already sent is recalled; the screens say so. App-first B07 adds explicit per-measurement source, observation time and canonical-unit MIS selectors with existing provenance fallback and server-pinned internal mapping revisions; reviewed impact/publication preserves approved wire bodies. D03 isolated routine candidate reuses current guided controls and four audited commands bound to one selected person;50 built synthetic backend/browser checks cover shared three-pack capture, provenance, lost committed acknowledgement, explicit conflict recovery and changed-authority refusal. Existing encrypted local component full-fixture p95 is separately measured under the unchanged500ms target; the connected screen does not acknowledge durable local work. Final CI/review/host gate and representative-device/worker/formal acceptance remain pending. See docs/current/d03/SPECIFICATION.md and EVIDENCE.json. Overnight recovery checkpoint (11 October 2026): originating organisation and unresolved exact-key continuity are repaired; a bounded audited higher-version/no-receipt disposition permits explicit stale recovery. Current source/evidence and the unresolved full-unit contention failure are in docs/current/d03/OVERNIGHT-RECOVERY-20261011.md. Local browser/component evidence is not full NFR, CI, hosted or formal acceptance. Overnight access/recovery checkpoint (11 October 2026): final consistent Routine/Today access checks, bounded ACCESS append liveness, original-organisation dispatch and original-account exact-attempt recovery have scoped native/browser and final203-unit/API/restore evidence. See docs/current/d03/OVERNIGHT-AUTHORITY-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUTHORITY.json. | BR-DATA-06 — Traceable amendments | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | Append-only revisions, linked amendments, voids and blocked stale plans. Slice 1c adds an identity-merge impact queue listing affected submissions, links, referrals, follow-ups, episodes, households and proxies. Slice 2j records, when a confirmed visit is amended or voided, one owned task per downstream output of that version (programme submissions, EHR publications, the plan, referrals, follow-ups, protocol evaluations, assistant results, shadow comparisons, worker activity, indicator release counts): work the server already refuses is recorded as invalidated, read-time computations as recomputed, the rest as review or correction tasks, shown in the action plan and exception queue and closed only with a verified receipt or a reason, audited. Slice 6ad (ninth review, finding 9): a visit with more than 60 downstream outputs can still be amended or voided; internal outputs past the cap are folded into one review task per kind with the count (every external output keeps its own task). Slice 13c: the shadow and drift reports recompute a corrected comparison on read (or leave it out for a void or an unconfirmed correction), so shadow-comparison correction tasks are recorded as recomputed or invalidated; released indicator figures stay open review tasks. App-first R27: one scoped correction/identity-merge reconciliation queue, audited current-scope assignment history and in-app acknowledgement notices, and deterministic historical backfill preview/apply with stale-plan and replay guards. App-first B27 captures immutable released populations, per-person counting material and pinned definitions; corrections leave original figures unchanged and prepare a parent-linked edition requiring independent review and current-authority publication. Derived reopened household follow-ups are counted consistently by live and newly frozen V3 material; historical editions and original completion fields remain unchanged. Frozen owner composite calculations retain canonical expression/examples and per-person primitive source lineage; supported void/reissue checks preserve original edition bytes and refuse unsafe dependency complements. Live/CSV post-audit revalidation refuses changed source/definition409. Wider impact discovery and protected reconstruction remain open. | Independent representative-scale validation, historical evidence completeness, external correction receipts, released indicator recomputation and formal acceptance remain pending. | BR-DATA-11 — Controlled migration | Should / R2 | partial / R15 | Legacy workspace migration validates counts, source hash and rollback; a controlled fixture-marking migration has an owner dry run with counts and rejects, applies only the reviewed plan with per-record lineage and an audit batch, and reverses a batch. Slice 2f: a controlled patient import: owners, supervisors and supervision delegates dry-run a CSV of people for their teams (accepted, rejected with reasons, possible duplicates against permitted records and earlier rows; ids and counts only) under a plan hash bound to the rows and record versions, then apply only that plan with lineage, a revision per person and one counted audit event. Slice 6p (independent review): a fixture batch reversal removes marks only from records in the reverting owner's care scope; others keep their mark, are reported and can be reversed by an owner whose scope covers them (each mark once). Slice 6q: the ASHA role no longer imports (her duplicate matching could not see other ASHAs' patients); the import file limit is 16,000 UTF-8 bytes. Slice 2h: the same importers dry-run CSVs of programme enrolments and external identifiers for existing people matched by a Saha record id or an exact recorded identifier (one neutral reason for unknown and out-of-scope records): episodes open on the adopted or a named compiled or activated pack version and an already open episode is refused; identifiers are format-checked for known issuers, ABHA is refused, and a value already on another record is refused, naming that record only when the importer can see it; apply takes only the plan with lineage, a revision per record and one counted audit event. Every import dry run (people, enrolments, identifiers) has a downloadable rejection report of row numbers and reasons only, formula-escaped, with a control total and SHA-256 manifest, recorded as an audited export. Slice 6aa: identifier import accepts listed issuers only (rch, nikshay) and refuses ABHA-shaped values under any issuer; the rejection report's audit event no longer moves the organisation revision. Slice 10i: an owner or a clinician (not an ASHA, an administrator or a supervisor who is not an owner) dry-runs a CSV of historical visits with clinical content, one row per visit, for people in their own normal care scope (owner authority and emergency access do not widen it): person by record id or recorded identifier (one neutral reason for unknown and out-of-scope records), visit date, source system, measurements with a stated unit and a source (measured or reported), an override reason and notes. Units are stated and converted by the slice 2i rules; a value outside the placeholder expected range needs a reason and one beyond the confirmation range is refused; a visit already imported from the same source system on the same date, or repeated in the file, is refused. The dry run lists the visits to create and the rows rejected with reasons, the SHA-256 of the file and a plan hash, and returns no clinical values or notes; apply takes only that plan and creates confirmed visits marked IMPORTED with lineage (file digest, row, importer, time) and no clinician review or sign-off implied, with a revision per visit and one counted audit event. An import never creates enrolments, submissions, referrals or plans and never changes the person; an imported visit is read-only history, kept out of sending, referral, publication, assistant summaries, FHIR download, indicators, reports, pilot and shadow measures, freshness and work statistics, so it is not reused as a current observation without a fresh measurement. The rejection report covers visits. The placeholder value ranges are not clinically approved. App-first B08 contacts dry-run/hash/apply retains source/file/row/batch provenance, always imports permission false and refuses overwriting care preferences. Reviewed contact-only reversal appends withdrawal and requires unchanged unconsented preferences; original actor current all-person/purpose authority applies to saved receipts. Actual phone recovery/export and independent consent/authority checks pass. App-first imported evidence adds pinned documentary answers, structured notes and bounded original media bytes, independent explicit source decisions, empty linked current drafts, same-date overlap review and retained batch withdrawals. Independent review repairs merged-original audited history, scoped saved-receipt recovery and phone overflow; no source facts become current automatically. | Wider import quality reports, issuer verification/correction, representative source contracts and larger media/history qualification remain. Documentary imported-source review and overlap decisions are delivered within declared limits; partner clinical-version conflict queues remain separate B09 work. Formal/clinical/external acceptance stays pending. | | BR-DATA-12 — Measurement of actual reuse | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Slice 2a: reuse opportunities, successful reuse and blocked reuse recomputed from stored confirmed visits, plus possible repeated entries and corrections, as versioned catalogue indicators with suppression. B26 adds owner-enabled common-fact capture-session active-time bands with monotonic foreground/idle exclusion, fixed self-reported help counts, and governed count-only weekly reporting. Saved clinical commands are independent of measurement failures. This is sampled session time, not comparable completed-visit total time or observed savings. Protected saved-mutation provenance now links online capture/correction/manual reconciliation stages and confirmed-visit totals, pinned programme mix and explicit incomplete coverage. Independent counter bands are excluded from comparable totals; all seconds remain client-reported. Opt-in field timing excludes background/idle intervals and uses serialized latest-book encrypted writes. Exact accepted mutation receipts bind field segments; current online continuation closes saved origins without fabricating unknown history. Migration0026 preserves populated rows and audit evidence while allowing multiple phase/session origins. | Baseline comparability, real transport recovery and external support workflows beyond the bounded internal helper implementation, validation against observed field practice and real pilot time savings remain unproven. | | BR-PROG-05 — Cross-programme scheduling | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Slice 1 adds a per-household view of each member's open items and a purpose-scoped sharing preview. Slice 10f adds a household visit plan (each in-scope member's open follow-ups, referrals and routine visit in one place; members outside the worker's scope are counted, never named) and household visit slots (householdVisitSchedule, householdVisitCancel) that cover chosen items of several members without changing any due date, owner or completion condition: an item stays open until its own record is resolved, items due before the slot date are shown as late, one item sits in one planned slot, and a slot holds only references, a date and a window (no free text). A confirmed visit's programme episodes can raise referral needs (createProgrammeReferrals): the same need, facility, person and visit become one referral that links every asking pack as a source and records which packs asked; a different need or facility stays a separate referral. Atomic original-planner rescheduling now preserves the prior slot and creates a linked replacement in one version-bound commit, checks every covered current person and purpose, and preserves due dates/owners/completion. Independent real Worker/phone review includes scope changes, role demotion, receipt/audit rollback, replay and slot-capacity checks. Reviewed explicit installed completion/referral declarations support selected covered-item visit lineage, independent compatibility and neutral slot-linked outreach; unmet and other-member work stays open, void derives unresolved work and replacement appends evidence. | Approved privacy and timing rules (the late flag, the one-slot-per-item rule and the need catalogue are local defaults); packs do not declare referral needs or compatibility, so the worker names the need and the server matches it; referrals created before slice 10f carry no need and are never merged; plans exist only for people in a household; reminders/outreach for a slot and completing covered items from the visit remain separate software; physical/clinical timing acceptance is pending. | | BR-PROG-08 — Additional programme packs | Should / R2 | partial / R16 | Slice 4f: an owner uploads a size-bounded, strictly validated data-only pack manifest (questions on shared facts with fixed units or options, show-when conditions, follow-up interval, escalation rules in the protocol rule vocabulary, declared mapping from earlier versions); conflicts with compiled and installed packs are refused; a clinician other than the uploader reviews the exact manifest hash; an owner activates; activation, review and retirement are versioned and audited, retirement never deletes; episodes open only on activated versions and slice 4e migration moves open episodes to an installed version through the declared mapping. Synthetic packs only; installed packs are marked not clinically approved. Expansion proposals still describe programmes without installing them. Slice 6z: question ids that name Object.prototype members (constructor, toString and similar) are refused by the manifest schema, and answers and absence reasons are read as own properties only, for installed and compiled packs. Optional installed reviewed completion/referral declarations pin exact rule/question/version/hash and do not invent rules for legacy compiled packs. | Clinically approved pack content and a clinical sign-off workflow; | BR-AG-07 — Limits and cancellation | Must / R1 | partial / R11 | One-call checkpointing, timeout/cancellation, per-capability daily call and reserved-token quotas; no automatic provider substitution. Slice 5a: monthly USD budgets per capability (estimates from recorded rates) checked before preparing and before sending; clinicians and supervisors with care scope can stop another worker's unsent request; sent requests are never reported as recalled. Slice 5e: per-capability task time and attempt limits in the reviewed manifest; unsent requests expire at the limit; a sent request without a result past the limit is shown as timed out with an uncertain outcome, never retried, and closed only by a recorded human decision; attempts beyond the limit refused before preparing and before sending; owner-owned budget alerts at 80% and 100% of estimated use. Slice 5h: owners record a provider invoice summary per provider and month and reconcile it against Saha's recorded estimates per model, with an owner-set threshold flag; records are append-only and audited, later ones supersede earlier ones, and budgets and rates are never changed automatically; owner-owned alerts for flagged variance and providers used without a reconciliation in the previous month. | A looping-provider stop reporting completed/cancelled/unresolved actions across multi-step tasks, background detection and delivery of timed-out requests, provider billing API reconciliation, delivered alerts and validated provider outage operation. | [lib/saha/agent.ts](lib/saha/agent.ts)<br>[lib/saha/agent-execution.ts](lib/saha/agent-execution.ts)<br>[tests/agent-contract.mjs](tests/agent-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/agent-smoke.mjs](tests/agent-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/governance.ts](lib/saha/governance.ts)<br>[tests/governance-smoke.mjs](tests/governance-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/governance-contract.mjs](tests/governance-contract.mjs)<br>[AI-GOVERNANCE.md](AI-GOVERNANCE.md)<br>[lib/saha/ai-budget.ts](lib/saha/ai-budget.ts)<br>[tests/ai-budget-contract.mjs](tests/ai-budget-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/ai-budget-smoke.mjs](tests/ai-budget-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-5A-AI-BUDGETS.md](docs/current/SLICE-5A-AI-BUDGETS.md)<br>[lib/saha/task-limits.ts](lib/saha/task-limits.ts)<br>[tests/task-limits-contract.mjs](tests/task-limits-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-5E-TASK-LIMITS-BUDGET-ALERTS.md](docs/current/SLICE-5E-TASK-LIMITS-BUDGET-ALERTS.md)<br>[lib/saha/ai-invoice.ts](lib/saha/ai-invoice.ts)<br>[tests/ai-invoice-contract.mjs](tests/ai-invoice-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/ai-invoice-smoke.mjs](tests/ai-invoice-smoke.mjs)<br>[app/ai-invoice-panel.tsx](app/ai-invoice-panel.tsx)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-5H-INVOICE-RECONCILIATION.md](docs/current/SLICE-5H-INVOICE-RECONCILIATION.md) |

| BR-AG-11 — Programme manager analysis | Should / R2 | partial / R15 | Slice 6d: a catalogue of eight versioned indicators queried over the viewer's scope with cohort minimum, small-cell and complementary suppression and freshness, plus a keyword interpreter that maps a question to catalogue entries for review without running it or using a model. Slice 6s: the catalogue is an owner-maintained registry of versioned definitions built from a fixed vocabulary of measures, filters and exclusions; the interpreter follows the registry; results name their definition version; one viewer's nested queries over seven days are checked for differencing and withheld when they would reveal a group of 1 to 4 people. Slice 6w: indicator queries are POST only (GET answers the catalogue and interpretation), a view no longer commits organisation-wide, and the CSV export is recorded as an access-audit event; differencing fixes as in BR-REP-04. Slice 6aa: indicator results carry the stated limits of small-group protection, and owners see them in the registry; the population-based differencing check withholds more results for small teams and narrow filters. | Natural-language analysis under governance, validated definitions with an approval workflow, owner-defined measures, and differencing protection across viewers, non-nested filters and groupings. | [REMAINING-RELEASES.md](REMAINING-RELEASES.md)<br>[lib/saha/indicators.ts](lib/saha/indicators.ts)<br>[lib/saha/indicator-registry.ts](lib/saha/indicator-registry.ts)<br>[lib/saha/indicator-differencing.ts](lib/saha/indicator-differencing.ts)<br>[lib/saha/indicators-server.ts](lib/saha/indicators-server.ts)<br>[tests/indicator-registry-contract.mjs](tests/indicator-registry-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/indicator-registry-smoke.mjs](tests/indicator-registry-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6S-INDICATOR-REGISTRY.md](docs/current/SLICE-6S-INDICATOR-REGISTRY.md)<br>[tests/fifth-review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/fifth-review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6W-FIFTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6W-FIFTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[tests/seventh-review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/seventh-review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6AA-SEVENTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6AA-SEVENTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md) |

| BR-AI-04 — Capability lifecycle | Must / R1 | partial / R11 | Draft/reviewed/active/suspended/retired revisions, replacement history and scoped affected-encounter views. Slice 5d: BRD lifecycle stages derived per package (registered, technically verified, approved for scope, active, suspended, retired, replaced), replacement recorded on activation, use counted per exact version, and in-progress work of an unusable capability identified with its required action (cancel, withheld, review, check applied values) on the governance page and in the audit line. Slice 5f: shadow operation of a reviewed revision for named teams or the whole organisation over at most 30 days; runs on confirmed visits (local rules provider only) without showing, applying or planning its output; consent, restriction, budget and lifecycle respected. Slice 7e: every programme destination, EHR connection and the mock ABDM gateway has owner-managed, versioned and audited create, update, delete or void, lookup and aggregate permissions, checked on the server before preparation and again inside each external-execution checkpoint; a withdrawal closes the item as not sent, shown on the trace and in the exception queue. | BR-INT-04 — Duplicate prevention and uncertain outcomes | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | Stable keys, lookup-before-resend and uncertain outcomes tested with mock transport. Slice 6u: an EHR publication refused before its write request (patient check or authority recheck) is closed as not sent ('blocked') instead of staying uncertain; one that may have been sent stays uncertain and needs a receipt lookup. Slice 7f: an owner may declare per EHR connection, from the partner contract, that its identifier search is authoritative with a consistency delay; an uncertain publication then resolves by lookup as accepted (one equal match), not stored after the delay (closed, never resent; a new publication only through the normal approval) or conflict (several or a different match); default off keeps the earlier rule. Each lookup checks its operation, is audited before sending, traced and limited to 24 per publication; the write must start within 30 s of the checkpoint. Replay soak: unresolved EHR share about 21% for the default partner and 0 for the authoritative one, no duplicate stored document. | Real partner duplicate/reconciliation contracts and timeout acceptance; partner evidence that its search is authoritative and its consistency delay holds. | [lib/saha/mis.ts](lib/saha/mis.ts)<br>[lib/saha/ehr.ts](lib/saha/ehr.ts)<br>[tests/mis-contract.mjs](tests/mis-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/ehr-contract.mjs](tests/ehr-contract.mjs)<br>[REMAINING-RELEASES.md](REMAINING-RELEASES.md)<br>[lib/saha/ehr-execution.ts](lib/saha/ehr-execution.ts)<br>[tests/ehr-execution.mjs](tests/ehr-execution.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6U-FOURTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6U-FOURTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[lib/saha/ehr-provider.ts](lib/saha/ehr-provider.ts)<br>[tests/ehr-resolution-contract.mjs](tests/ehr-resolution-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/replay-soak-contract.mjs](tests/replay-soak-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-7F-EHR-RESOLUTION.md](docs/current/SLICE-7F-EHR-RESOLUTION.md) |

| BR-INT-09 — Per-destination data minimisation | Must / R1 | partial / R12 | Per-destination mapped field allowlists. Slice 7e: aggregate-only programme destinations refuse every patient-level record before preparation and inside the delivery checkpoint. Slice 7g: an aggregate-only demonstration destination receives approved aggregate reports only: suppressed cells from named indicator versions, a definition and version manifest, row count and SHA-256; a payload minimisation check (no patient-level field or identifier value, no released cell from 1 to 4) runs before preparing, before the checkpoint and inside it, and its result is recorded on the report; a failed check closes the report as not sent. Slice 6ab: aggregate report screening also uses the slice 6aa population check (preparation, pre-send recomputation and checkpoint), and a ledger write D1 refuses closes the report as not sent. Slice 6ad (ninth review): aggregate report reads return the manifest and withheld blocks but never the cells (figures go to the destination only); payloads are kept only while a report is open and bounded (150 kB each, 450 kB for all reports beside the 1 MB ledger); only open reports count towards the limit of 20 and unapproved or unsent reports expire after 24 hours; a late destination answer is appended as evidence; receipt lookup stays available in maintenance and the send checkpoint re-checks maintenance. Patient-level destination envelopes now constrain paths/fields/current mapping and exact approved digest, and repeat sensitive checks before immediate send. Aggregate malformed arrays fail closed and suppression repeats even for a rehashed stored body. Validated FHIR headers are materialised without losing conditional-create fields; server credentials are added last, caller credentials refused, uncertain lookup remains body-free. | Live destination contracts, representative real-profile minimisation and partner testing; synthetic boundary evidence does not complete all destination-specific semantics. | | BR-INT-14 — ABHA identity and record linking | Must / R1 | partial / R13 | Slice 7a: ABHA link after a mock one-time-code check, decline, unlink with history and consent revocation, one current record per ABHA number, masked display. Local mock gateway only. Slice 6r: patient history is redacted on read (ABHA numbers masked in revision data and field changes, received bundles withheld); stored revisions unchanged. Slice 6u: when the record already holding an ABHA number is outside the worker's scope, the link refusal is neutral and does not reveal it. Slice 6z: that refusal is now the ordinary link refusal (same status and text as a gateway refusal), and the server records the conflict as an audit event on the holding record for an identity steward; the requester is not told. Slice 6ab: an ABHA held by an out-of-scope record is refused before the one-time code is looked at; because the mock code is public the refusal is still distinguishable from a link, so the oracle is bounded (one steward review event per requester and holding record a day; after three in a day every further link by that requester is refused the same way), not removed. Slice 10e: an ABHA number is accepted as 14 digits or grouped 2-4-4-4 with hyphens and refused otherwise, in the link command and in search. No checksum is applied because no official specification of one was found: the PIB explainer and community guides call it a random 14-digit number, and abdm.gov.in and the sandbox documents could not be read from the build environment. ABHA review reservations apply before linking lookups, including audit-failure retention and rollout coverage; no live ABDM acceptance is implied. | Authorised ABHA creation and verification with ABDM, a checksum if ABDM specifies one, issuer checks, and correction through ABDM. | ## BR-OFF

| Requirement | Priority / BRD phase | Assessment / target | Current behaviour | Remaining work | Related evidence context |

| BR-OFF-01 — Defined offline capability | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Encrypted bounded draft notebook with unavailable online functions. Slice 3g: new people, first-visit facts and referral requests can be captured offline and wait in the encrypted notebook until sync. App-first A02 adds a public locked offline /app shell sharing the unchanged encrypted field vault: current prepared visits, capture and sync; account/packet-owner/lifetime gates, ciphertext-only storage and public-only caches; weight and notes survive actual offline browser reload. Synced visits remain unconfirmed until online review. The app view does not claim a full offline caseload or approved-guidance cache. App-only connected patient-scope revalidation and redirect refusal are independently browser-tested; prepared-visit scope remains narrower than a complete offline caseload. A05 adds signed prepared documentary task types and complete captured programme pack/version guidance, encrypted offline conditional answers and explicit absence reasons. New server-held work digests and captured episode/version checks apply before sync even if an older client omits its optional digest. Sync remains an unconfirmed draft; clinical approval remains pending. | Cached approved guidance, wider task capture and actual offline device acceptance. | [lib/saha/field.ts](lib/saha/field.ts)<br>[tests/field-contract.mjs](tests/field-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/field-client.mjs](tests/field-client.mjs)<br>[tests/field-shell.mjs](tests/field-shell.mjs)<br>[public/field/app.js](public/field/app.js)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-3G-OFFLINE-REGISTRATION.md](docs/current/SLICE-3G-OFFLINE-REGISTRATION.md)<br>[tests/field-registration-client.mjs](tests/field-registration-client.mjs)<br>[public/app-sw.js](public/app-sw.js)<br>[public/app/offline/index.html](public/app/offline/index.html)<br>[public/field/app-grants.js](public/field/app-grants.js)<br>[tests/app-offline-browser.mjs](tests/app-offline-browser.mjs)<br>[tests/app-offline-contract.mjs](tests/app-offline-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/APP-SHARED-OFFLINE.md](docs/current/APP-SHARED-OFFLINE.md)<br>[tests/app-offline-review-browser.mjs](tests/app-offline-review-browser.mjs)<br>[tests/app-offline-scope-browser.mjs](tests/app-offline-scope-browser.mjs)<br>[tests/app-offline-scope-contract.mjs](tests/app-offline-scope-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/app-sw-review-contract.mjs](tests/app-sw-review-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/APP-OFFLINE-INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md](docs/current/APP-OFFLINE-INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md)<br>[lib/saha/programmes.ts](lib/saha/programmes.ts)<br>[tests/offline-programme-contract.mjs](tests/offline-programme-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/offline-programme-browser.mjs](tests/offline-programme-browser.mjs)<br>[docs/current/APP-OFFLINE-PROGRAMME.md](docs/current/APP-OFFLINE-PROGRAMME.md)<br>[docs/current/OFFLINE-PROGRAMME-INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md](docs/current/OFFLINE-PROGRAMME-INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md) |

| BR-OFF-05 — Safe synchronisation ordering | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Stable-key sync for existing authorised person and visit drafts. Slice 3g: a person registered offline, with a first visit draft and referral request, is created in one command keyed by a stable registration id, so resends under new keys never create a second person; Sync all sends registrations, then visit drafts, then media, stops on an uncertain outcome and retries it with the same key; tested with repeated lost responses. Slice 3h: a clinician turns the synced request into a referral in one step, or declines it, once the visit is confirmed. Slice 3i: open synced requests are listed for clinicians and supervisors until decided. | Physical-device testing across real connection loss, and background sync. | [lib/saha/field.ts](lib/saha/field.ts)<br>[tests/field-contract.mjs](tests/field-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/field-client.mjs](tests/field-client.mjs)<br>[tests/field-shell.mjs](tests/field-shell.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/field-registration.ts](lib/saha/field-registration.ts)<br>[public/field/app.js](public/field/app.js)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-3G-OFFLINE-REGISTRATION.md](docs/current/SLICE-3G-OFFLINE-REGISTRATION.md)<br>[tests/field-registration-client.mjs](tests/field-registration-client.mjs)<br>[tests/field-registration-smoke.mjs](tests/field-registration-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/referral-request.ts](lib/saha/referral-request.ts)<br>[app/referral-request-panel.tsx](app/referral-request-panel.tsx)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-3H-REFERRAL-REQUESTS.md](docs/current/SLICE-3H-REFERRAL-REQUESTS.md)<br>[tests/referral-request-smoke.mjs](tests/referral-request-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/referral-request-queue.ts](lib/saha/referral-request-queue.ts)<br>[app/referral-request-queue-panel.tsx](app/referral-request-queue-panel.tsx)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-3I-REFERRAL-REQUEST-QUEUE.md](docs/current/SLICE-3I-REFERRAL-REQUEST-QUEUE.md)<br>[tests/referral-request-queue-contract.mjs](tests/referral-request-queue-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/referral-request-queue-smoke.mjs](tests/referral-request-queue-smoke.mjs) |

A05 adds signed prepared documentary task types and complete captured programme pack/version guidance, encrypted offline conditional answers and explicit absence reasons. New server-held work digests and captured episode/version checks apply before sync even if an older client omits its optional digest. | BR-CARE-01 — Action ownership | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Follow-up/referral/outreach owners; R10 unavailable-owner queue and handover. Slice 4b: per-patient action plan shows each open item's owner or responsible queue and flags items without a current eligible owner; a care worker takes ownership of an unowned follow-up or referral, and a supervisor or owner of the patient's team reassigns them. Slice 4d: a clinician with current access or a steward of the patient's team records that one follow-up or referral waits on another open item of the same patient; the plan shows each waiting item's blocker with the blocker's owner state and status, and what each item holds back. | All clinical/integration exceptions, urgency policy and production monitoring coverage; queue notification; safety-case reassignment outside clinicians. | [lib/saha/continuity.ts](lib/saha/continuity.ts)<br>[lib/saha/outreach.ts](lib/saha/outreach.ts)<br>[lib/saha/field.ts](lib/saha/field.ts)<br>[tests/continuity-smoke.mjs](tests/continuity-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/outreach-smoke.mjs](tests/outreach-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/governance.ts](lib/saha/governance.ts)<br>[tests/governance-smoke.mjs](tests/governance-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/governance-contract.mjs](tests/governance-contract.mjs)<br>[AI-GOVERNANCE.md](AI-GOVERNANCE.md)<br>[lib/saha/action-plan.ts](lib/saha/action-plan.ts)<br>[tests/action-plan-contract.mjs](tests/action-plan-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/action-plan-smoke.mjs](tests/action-plan-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-4B-ACTION-PLAN.md](docs/current/SLICE-4B-ACTION-PLAN.md)<br>[lib/saha/referrals.ts](lib/saha/referrals.ts)<br>[app/action-plan-panel.tsx](app/action-plan-panel.tsx)<br>[app/referral-panel.tsx](app/referral-panel.tsx)<br>[tests/plan-dependencies-contract.mjs](tests/plan-dependencies-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/plan-dependencies-smoke.mjs](tests/plan-dependencies-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-4D-ACTION-DEPENDENCIES.md](docs/current/SLICE-4D-ACTION-DEPENDENCIES.md) |

| BR-CARE-03 — Referral lifecycle | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Dated referral events with evidence. SAHA-101 locally distinguishes proposed, authorised, sent, received, scheduled, attended and completed states; completion requires prior attendance, a coded service outcome, evidence source/reference and the recording actor. Declined, cancelled, not-attended and unknown dispositions remain distinct; closed states are immutable. | Facility-verified outcomes, partner integration, formal QA and observed external outcome evidence. | [lib/saha/continuity.ts](lib/saha/continuity.ts)<br>[lib/saha/outreach.ts](lib/saha/outreach.ts)<br>[lib/saha/field.ts](lib/saha/field.ts)<br>[tests/continuity-smoke.mjs](tests/continuity-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/outreach-smoke.mjs](tests/outreach-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/governance.ts](lib/saha/governance.ts)<br>[tests/governance-smoke.mjs](tests/governance-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/governance-contract.mjs](tests/governance-contract.mjs)<br>[AI-GOVERNANCE.md](AI-GOVERNANCE.md) |
| BR-CARE-04 — Due work and escalation | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Due dates and overdue counts; scoped handover exception queue. Slice 4a adds urgency, reasoned delays with constraint and history, and a computed escalation queue ordered by level, urgency and days overdue. Slice 3i: a queue of open field referral requests for clinicians, supervisors and owners in scope (clinical concern first, then oldest, with waiting time and a link to the visit); requests waiting 48 hours or more are also exception-queue rows. Slice 4d: in the action plan an item waiting on an open blocker is not counted as overdue and is listed after other work; an overdue blocker keeps its own place, and an ownership gap still escalates a waiting item. Slice 0b-4: in a workspace paged for a scope above 500 people, the follow-up list (due dates and overdue marks) and the referral tracker on My work cover the viewer's whole scope, built on the server page by page with the same order and counts as the unpaged workspace; before, they covered only opened records. Slice 10b: the overdue-referral, overdue-follow-up and undated-referral alerts can be sent out of the app to a listed recipient tier as simulated messages carrying a count and a link only, and an unacknowledged alert escalates to the next tier after an owner-set interval; every message has a receipt and an audit event (demonstration simulator in synthetic and training environments only). | Approved urgency policy, delivery of real notifications to approved recipients, on-call escalation, and automatic reassignment. The referral escalation queue, caseload and handover counts do not yet apply action dependencies. | | BR-CARE-05 — Unified action plan | Must / R1 | partial / R14 | Shared visit facts and duplicate patient/day reminder suppression. Slice 4b: one ordered per-patient action plan across programme follow-ups, referrals, protocol reviews, safety cases and outreach, each with its source; follow-ups with the same title, due date and owner from several programme episodes shown once with every source. Slice 4d: recorded, audited dependencies between one patient's follow-ups, referrals and protocol reviews (self, cycle, closed-item and cross-patient links refused; removal needs a reason and keeps the record); a referral links further visits, protocol evaluations and programme episodes of the same patient, each with its kind and time, shown in the referral view and the action plan; single-source referrals are unchanged. Slice 10f: a referral can be created from several programme packs at once (createProgrammeReferrals): programme episodes on one confirmed visit that raise the same need for the same facility share one referral that links each asking episode as a source (slice 4d) and records which packs asked; the action plan names the asking packs on the referral's sources. Slice 13d: the household view records one read-audit event per member shown. | No dependency is derived automatically; packs do not declare their own referral needs (the worker names the need and the server matches it), referrals made before slice 10f carry no need and are never merged, and the need catalogue is a local default; programme pack migration and installation; dependencies are not applied to reminders, the escalation queue or caseload counts. | [lib/saha/continuity.ts](lib/saha/continuity.ts)<br>[lib/saha/outreach.ts](lib/saha/outreach.ts)<br>[lib/saha/field.ts](lib/saha/field.ts)<br>[tests/continuity-smoke.mjs](tests/continuity-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/outreach-smoke.mjs](tests/outreach-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/governance.ts](lib/saha/governance.ts)<br>[tests/governance-smoke.mjs](tests/governance-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/governance-contract.mjs](tests/governance-contract.mjs)<br>[AI-GOVERNANCE.md](AI-GOVERNANCE.md)<br>[lib/saha/action-plan.ts](lib/saha/action-plan.ts)<br>[tests/action-plan-contract.mjs](tests/action-plan-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/action-plan-smoke.mjs](tests/action-plan-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-4B-ACTION-PLAN.md](docs/current/SLICE-4B-ACTION-PLAN.md)<br>[lib/saha/referrals.ts](lib/saha/referrals.ts)<br>[app/action-plan-panel.tsx](app/action-plan-panel.tsx)<br>[app/referral-panel.tsx](app/referral-panel.tsx)<br>[tests/plan-dependencies-contract.mjs](tests/plan-dependencies-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/plan-dependencies-smoke.mjs](tests/plan-dependencies-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-4D-ACTION-DEPENDENCIES.md](docs/current/SLICE-4D-ACTION-DEPENDENCIES.md)<br>[lib/saha/referral-consolidation.ts](lib/saha/referral-consolidation.ts)<br>[lib/saha/referral-needs.ts](lib/saha/referral-needs.ts)<br>[app/programme-referral-panel.tsx](app/programme-referral-panel.tsx)<br>[tests/household-visit-contract.mjs](tests/household-visit-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/household-visit-smoke.mjs](tests/household-visit-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-10F-HOUSEHOLD-SCHEDULING.md](docs/current/SLICE-10F-HOUSEHOLD-SCHEDULING.md)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-13D-REVIEW-LEFTOVERS.md](docs/current/SLICE-13D-REVIEW-LEFTOVERS.md) |
| BR-CARE-06 — Safe communication preferences | Must / R1 | implemented-local / R14 | Per-person, purpose and channel contact preferences now retain bounded ordered before/after change history, explicit phone ownership, preferred language, local contact window, permission, private-contact choice and a disclosure limit. | BR-CARE-08 — Worker activity evidence | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Visit-backed outreach completion and correction-aware evidence. Slice 4c: per-worker summary for supervisors separating performed, claimed, verified (evidence-matched) and submitted activity, with corrections traced to reviser and reason and no payment language or location. Slice 10c: worker incentive claims on an owner-set synthetic placeholder schedule (not a real rate): an ASHA prepares a claim from her own current confirmed visits and outreach completions, each line linked to its source record and never claimed twice; the team supervisor verifies or returns it with a reason; a different owner approves; an approved claim is immutable and stored with a digest chained to the previous claim, with corrections as new linked claims and a CSV export carrying the digests; no payment is made or implied. ## BR-REP

| Requirement | Priority / BRD phase | Assessment / target | Current behaviour | Remaining work | Related evidence context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BR-REP-01 — Operational exception queues | Must / R1 | implemented-local / R15 | Operational backlog counts plus the established item queue, trace, alert and redacted device-report foundations. SAHA-107 local completion adds explicit scoped identity-conflict and append-only fixed-code device-diagnostic rows; versioned assignment from every visible row with current viewer/assignee scope, stale-row and race refusal, bounded history and audit; a metadata-only stable device diagnostic outbox for offline or not-yet-attributed failures that drains after trusted reconnect; and opaque one-occurrence-per-item demonstration notification planning. Assignment never executes recovery, retries delivery, merges identities or changes clinical records. Focused synthetic contract, device, notification, TypeScript and production-build evidence is recorded in docs/current/SAHA-107-OPERATIONAL-EXCEPTION-QUEUES.md. | Formal TC-BR-REP-01-A/B execution with the controlled FX-REP-01/NEG fixtures, independent review, exact-source CI, physical-device failure/loss validation, approved live notification-provider acceptance, merge and observed deployment remain pending. | | BR-REP-02 — Governed indicator definitions | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Fixed operational definitions and documented denominators/window. Slice 6d adds eight versioned indicator definitions with numerator, denominator, exclusions, owner and cadence. Slice 6s makes them version 1 entries of an owner-maintained, append-only registry: owners add, revise and retire definitions (measures, filters, exclusions, owner, cadence, minimum cohort) with a reason and audit event; every result names the definition and version used, and a recorded version can be run again to reproduce an earlier result over current records. App-first B27 definition changes are proposals with immutable events, distinct active supervisor/clinician review and current-owner dated release. Only released effective versions enter catalogue or explicit queries; rollback/retirement are new reviewed proposals. Current governance clock is independent of historical reporting cutoff, verified on actual aggregate preparation; withdrawn future releases stay refused. Legacy definitions remain explicitly historical, without fabricated approval. App-first B27 stores immutable definition/window/population material and digest, distinct current-manager review and original-author publication. Counting-source coverage version2 includes supported submission cancellation; stale review/publication refuses without accepted events or receipts, while prior published editions remain unchanged. Current reviewed V3 source coverage rejects stale old prepared editions while preserving original published edition bytes; protected operational export remains metadata-only and full reconstruction is still a software residual. Reviewed composite extension accepts a strict bounded per-person expression over the fixed primitive vocabulary, with2–6 owner-entered examples, safe integer bounds, explicit independent review and dated effective release. Live/CSV/frozen results retain exact operand/examples/governance lineage; original primitive operands remain compatible. Independently cleared current-authority repair rechecks reporting permission, original care scope and pinned-time figures after final audit. This bounded software contract does not approve clinical denominators or arbitrary formula code. | Owner-defined measure expressions, validation against clinical programme specifications, immutable point-in-time released population snapshots and controlled correction reissues remain active; formal denominator/clinical approval remains pending. Canonical frozen edition regressions now cover bounded stored-material/reissue and supported cancellation software; broader downstream lineage, governed archival/capacity and official publication validation remain pending. | | BR-REP-03 — Data freshness and completeness | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Generation window and usage coverage limitations are visible. Slice 6d indicator results state computation time, window, cohort size and latest source record. Slice 6ac adds, per team the viewer belongs to, the latest record time and banded counts of records arriving in the window and arriving more than 72 hours late for online visits, field-notebook syncs, imports and programme receipts, a late share to the nearest 10 points when at least 20 records support it, a data-still-syncing warning for field grants unsynced after 12 hours, and on each released visit-based result the banded number of late visits confirmed since the viewer's previous release of that figure; none of it enters a released cell. App-first B28 adds reviewed exact UTC periods/deadlines, per-source lateness/sync thresholds, explicit declaration-only completeness pinned to intended actor and current source version, original/current frozen sidecars and legacy unknown-source-version handling. Whole-team reporting-purpose checks and exact read-only original receipt recovery preserve uncertain outcomes. Combined root419 focused assertions/build/types pass; operational0030 governance portability is independently cleared with private snapshot bodies omitted. | Current privacy-preserving source-completeness contract is partial: late arrivals for wider non-visit/source types and affected downstream released figures beyond the bounded stored edition contract, freshness on all aggregate/operational exports, configured official scheduled submissions, controlled purpose/download access, protected snapshot reconstruction and clinical/source acceptance remain. Full integration/hosted gates remain pending; no denominator is inferred from operator coverage. | [lib/saha/operations.ts](lib/saha/operations.ts)<br>[lib/saha/pilot.ts](lib/saha/pilot.ts)<br>[tests/operations-smoke.mjs](tests/operations-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/indicators.ts](lib/saha/indicators.ts)<br>[lib/saha/indicator-freshness.ts](lib/saha/indicator-freshness.ts)<br>[lib/saha/indicators-server.ts](lib/saha/indicators-server.ts)<br>[tests/indicator-freshness-contract.mjs](tests/indicator-freshness-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/indicator-freshness-smoke.mjs](tests/indicator-freshness-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/indicators-smoke.mjs](tests/indicators-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6AC-REPORTING-FRESHNESS.md](docs/current/SLICE-6AC-REPORTING-FRESHNESS.md)<br>[lib/saha/reporting-completeness.ts](lib/saha/reporting-completeness.ts)<br>[lib/saha/reporting-completeness-server.ts](lib/saha/reporting-completeness-server.ts)<br>[tests/integration-reporting-completeness-current-contract.mjs](tests/integration-reporting-completeness-current-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/reporting-completeness-repaired-http.mjs](tests/reporting-completeness-repaired-http.mjs)<br>[tests/integration-completeness-extra-authority-http.mjs](tests/integration-completeness-extra-authority-http.mjs)<br>[tests/integration-completeness-extra-browser.mjs](tests/integration-completeness-extra-browser.mjs)<br>[docs/current/REPORTING-COMPLETENESS-AUTHORITY-REPAIR.md](docs/current/REPORTING-COMPLETENESS-AUTHORITY-REPAIR.md)<br>[docs/current/REPORTING-PORTABILITY-ROOT-COLD-REVIEW.md](docs/current/REPORTING-PORTABILITY-ROOT-COLD-REVIEW.md) |
| BR-REP-04 — Privacy-preserving aggregation | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Current team scope and administrator patient-aggregate denial. Slice 6d indicator queries refuse cohorts under 5 and suppress small and complementary cells. Slice 6s adds per-definition minimum cohorts, differencing protection across one viewer's nested queries over seven days (hashed filters and per-cell people counts only, no patient data), and a CSV of indicator results that keeps the suppression; slice 6s-2 gives that CSV an export identifier, a row-count and SHA-256 manifest and an audit event. Slice 6w (fifth independent review): differencing compares numerators, denominators and denominator minus numerator as well as people; definition filters (sex, age band, programme) are nesting dimensions, so complementary definitions, including several in one query, are compared; the ledger is pruned by time only and refuses new queries at a per-viewer (400) or organisation (4,000) bound instead of evicting; a view moves no revision and writes no command receipt, queries are POST only, and data exit leaves the ledger out. Slice 6aa (seventh independent review): differencing is also checked through the people behind each release rather than by key: every measure release of the viewer's last seven days is re-evaluated against the people in scope now and split into atoms (people, and window-to-window increments for windowed measures, in exactly the same shown cells); a result that would create or split an atom of 1 to 4 is withheld, so a different grouping, an exclusion definition or a different window no longer bypasses it; the window is part of each release's identity; the ledger is bounded by size (1 MB organisation, 250 KB per viewer, 429 when full); a property test over random synthetic query sequences finds no released nested pair differing by 1 to 4; the remaining limits (two viewers combining results, changes between queries, releases older than seven days, unrelated measures) are stated to owners. Slice 6ad (ninth review): a release for a window that ended in the past records its end, used in the window key, the ledger prune identity and the population check, which counts each window to its own end and falls back to whole-person profiles when windows do not nest. Slice 13d: refused indicator queries (combination or cohort below 5) are recorded with filter names only and limited to 20 per viewer per day. App-first B27 protects frozen edition reads with current reporting purpose and entire original cohort scope, existing viewer disclosure guards, and durable comparison of actual contributions across previously disclosed editions; small changed groups are withheld and concurrent disclosure registration is guarded. Operational report export refuses raw units/disclosure state and resealed unsupported metadata/history; current cohort/purpose after-audit gates remain. This does not replace full protected reconstruction or full owner-expression privacy work. Composite privacy screening retains every primitive dependency and actual joint cohort membership profile, including zero contributors, across current/frozen and destination releases. Independent current-authority tests refuse late-audit reporting-purpose/programme withdrawal403 and supported clinical/definition changes409 without returning former figures. Existing cross-viewer/collusion and operating-policy residuals remain. | Differencing across viewers, non-nested filters, groupings and definitions, suppression on the operational export and other report paths, and a separate purpose for patient exports. | | BR-REP-05 — Reconciled exports | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Audited scoped CSV with generation time/window and formula escaping. Slice 6k: the CSV names its definition and version, export identifier, draft status, exporting member and role, window and teams; control totals tie displayed measures to the sums of their detail rows; a trailing manifest gives the row count and SHA-256 of the content, also recorded in the export's audit line and response headers; administrators and care roles cannot download it. Slice 7g: an aggregate report for an aggregate-only destination is prepared from named definition versions, a period and teams in the preparer's scope, approved by a different authorised person and sent with the same suppression, a manifest (definitions, row count, SHA-256), a release recorded for the destination in the differencing ledger, and the receipt kept in the report history on the destination's screen. Slice 6ab: aggregate reports are screened with the population check, as indicator queries are. Slice 6ad (ninth review): aggregate report cells are never shown to people (they were screened for the destination only); a report period ending in the past records its window end so it never replaces or escapes an earlier release to the same destination. Slice 13d: the send checkpoint applies the sender's scope before the population screen (it saw an empty population before). | Protected expiry of downloaded files, filters beyond window and team, and an official reviewed-submission lifecycle distinct from draft export. | BR-REP-06 — Population prioritisation | Should / R2 | implemented-local / R14 | Governed operational care-gap criteria with independent configuration review, visible limitations and uncertainty, scoped supervisor selection, reasoned human inclusion/allocation overrides, stale authority/source invalidation, small-cohort refusal and draft-only outreach. B26 adds default-disabled count-only sampling, atomic metadata receipts/counters/chained audit, current care authority/configuration guards and separately labelled parsed sync response categories. Weekly human care-active denominator uses durable trusted command-authority events; saved receipt checks do not prove transport recovery. An encrypted fixed-counter outbox retains UUIDs across reload/lost ACK and binds expected originating account to trusted server identity. Current policy reads are audited only when enabled, freeze the resolved organisation and recheck membership/configuration. Protected internal requests, scoped helper assignment, distinct-helper attestation and requester acknowledgement append exact workflow history; actual clinical mutation receipts link bounded online work stages. | Full encrypted field lifecycle instrumentation and timing continuity are in progress. Proven real uncertain transport-to-receipt recovery, external ticket linkage, comparable observed baseline and pilot evaluation remain. Internal synthetic intervention evidence is not external help-desk acceptance. | | BR-OPS-01 — Deployment configuration | Must / R1 | implemented-local / R15 | SAHA-114 adds a tenant-scoped, versioned deployment configuration lifecycle. An organisation owner stages exact organisation, geography, facility, role, programme, language, provider-eligibility and operational-limit settings; a different current deployment operator independently reviews the exact source-bound revision; a different current owner activates it only after all references and authority are revalidated. Active settings enforce role, programme, language, provider, location, member, patient-record and external-connection boundaries on current command paths. Configuration history and audit are retained, no provider is activated by configuration, and one tenant record cannot affect another. Synthetic contract34/34, TypeScript and production build pass on 20 October 2026. | Formal TC-BR-OPS-01-A/B remain not run; named human/domain approval, exact-source CI, protected merge and observed hosted deployment remain separate. | [lib/saha/operations.ts](lib/saha/operations.ts)<br>[lib/saha/pilot.ts](lib/saha/pilot.ts)<br>[tests/operations-smoke.mjs](tests/operations-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/pilot-smoke.mjs](tests/pilot-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/environment.ts](lib/saha/environment.ts)<br>[lib/saha/environment-server.ts](lib/saha/environment-server.ts)<br>[app/environment-panel.tsx](app/environment-panel.tsx)<br>[tests/environment-contract.mjs](tests/environment-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/environment-smoke.mjs](tests/environment-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6N-ENVIRONMENTS.md](docs/current/SLICE-6N-ENVIRONMENTS.md)<br>[tests/availability-contract.mjs](tests/availability-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/ui/availability.mjs](tests/ui/availability.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SYNTHETIC-AVAILABILITY.md](docs/current/SYNTHETIC-AVAILABILITY.md)<br>[lib/saha/deployment-configuration.ts](lib/saha/deployment-configuration.ts)<br>[lib/saha/deployment-configuration-server.ts](lib/saha/deployment-configuration-server.ts)<br>[app/api/deployment-configuration/route.ts](app/api/deployment-configuration/route.ts)<br>[app/deployment-configuration/page.tsx](app/deployment-configuration/page.tsx)<br>[app/deployment-configuration/screen.tsx](app/deployment-configuration/screen.tsx)<br>[tests/deployment-configuration-contract.mjs](tests/deployment-configuration-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/evidence/saha-114-deployment-configuration/result.json](tests/evidence/saha-114-deployment-configuration/result.json)<br>[docs/current/SAHA-114-DEPLOYMENT-CONFIGURATION.md](docs/current/SAHA-114-DEPLOYMENT-CONFIGURATION.md) |
