| BR-OPS-04 — Operational monitoring | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Hourly request metrics, incidents, queue counts and cost assumptions. Slice 6g: versioned alert rules computed on each operations read, each with an owning role, count and next safe action, scoped to the viewer. Slice 6h: the owning role acknowledges an alert with a note; the acknowledgement lapses when the count changes or after 24 hours. Slice 5e: owner-owned AI budget alerts (80% and 100% of estimated monthly use) in alert rules 1.1.0. Slice 5h: alert rules 1.2.0 add owner-owned alerts for AI invoices of the previous month that differ from Saha's estimates beyond the owner's threshold, and for providers used that month without a recorded reconciliation. Slice 6v: each alert that opens is delivered in-app (top-bar bell with unread count) to the members who own it, from their own scope, with its delivery time; an acknowledgement clears it; an occurrence left unacknowledged past its severity threshold (critical 30 minutes, high 4 hours, normal 24 hours), or re-opened after its acknowledgement stopped applying, escalates once to owners inside the organisation with a recorded reason; evaluated idempotently on reads and organisation-wide writes. Slice 6z: an organisation acknowledgement clears only the notifications of recipients whose own view shows it (counts differ by care scope); the occurrence counts as acknowledged, and is not escalated, only when every recipient's view shows it; escalation notifications are written only with the guarded escalation update, and restore never re-opens a closed occurrence. Slice 10b: an owner keeps a recipient list of named organisation members (owners, administrators, supervisors) with a tier (1 to 3), a channel (SMS or email) and the alert kinds each receives, and sets the number of minutes (5 to 1440) after which an alert that is still unacknowledged is sent to the next tier. A demonstration simulator (mode demo; synthetic and training environments only, refused with 409 elsewhere) stores each message with a simulated delivery receipt and a hash-chained audit event in one commit, one message per alert occurrence, tier and recipient; recipients hold no phone number or address and nothing leaves Saha. Messages carry the alert title, severity, a count and a path link only, never a patient name or identifier (counts of care-scoped alerts are withheld from owners outside that scope). Escalation is evaluated inside the alert delivery evaluation (alert and Operations reads, organisation-wide commands), as a pure planner that a scheduled trigger can call; the owner screen on Organisation shows the list, open alerts with their tier schedule and the message log with lateness. Slice 10a: the same scheduled job evaluates every organisation's alerts and in-app delivery about every five minutes whether or not anyone uses Saha (at most 20 organisations, 800 database statements and 20 seconds a run; organisations with due work first, the rest in rotation), through the slice 6v writer, and records the evaluation time and counts in the organisation record with an audit event naming the scheduled job (first evaluation, on any change, and at least hourly); in-app only, nothing is sent outside Saha. Verified in contract tests and a local scheduled-handler check, not on Cloudflare. Slice 13d: the scheduled rotation continues from a stored cursor, and the job's records are written without moving the organisation revision (no conflict with a person's command). | Delivery to real approved recipients (a live SMS or email provider, contact details, consent and message review), approved detection/response targets and escalation ladders; the scheduled evaluation has not run on the hosted Worker, needs the Workers Paid plan and its five-minute interval is a setting, not a measured detection time; on a host with no trigger, evaluation still depends on traffic. | | BR-OPS-07 — Data exit and continuity | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Local encrypted backup, configuration export, limited report/FHIR exports and an owner-only organisation export archive (every table, audit chain, media, manifest and checksums, hand-over list of unresolved external actions and owned patient tasks) with an offline verifier. Slice 6p (independent review): patient-linked entries inside organisation documents (safety cases, shadow results, outreach items and messages, person holds) now follow the owner's care scope and are counted as withheld (before 6p they were exported whole); a command saved during an export no longer discards it (the audit event is retried on a fresh read with the authority re-checked). Slice 6q: the audit-chain and operations export retries refuse (409, nothing delivered) a changed care scope and, for the operations CSV, a changed reporting reach. Slice 6u: household members and caseload transfer patients outside the exporter's scope are left out of those rows and counted, and the verifier checks them; pilot dossier and expansion pack exports retry their audit commit with access rechecked. Slice 6z: alert notifications are exported for the exporter only; other members' notifications (counts from their own care scope) are left out and counted in the manifest. SAHA-120 local successor adds a fail-closed whole-organisation mode that requires existing normal authority for every person, source-bound staged parts, explicit per-connection termination rehearsal disposition, value-free field units/provenance and a non-destructive validation re-import command; see docs/current/SAHA-120-DATA-EXIT-LOCAL-CODING-COMPLETION.md. | Independent operation of the 1000-person rehearsal, representative large-tenant capacity, buyer approval of the format, exact-source CI/review and formal acceptance. | [lib/saha/operations.ts](lib/saha/operations.ts)<br>[lib/saha/pilot.ts](lib/saha/pilot.ts)<br>[tests/operations-smoke.mjs](tests/operations-smoke.mjs)<br>[tests/pilot-smoke.mjs](tests/pilot-smoke.mjs)<br>[lib/saha/data-exit.ts](lib/saha/data-exit.ts)<br>[lib/saha/data-exit-server.ts](lib/saha/data-exit-server.ts)<br>[scripts/verify-export.mjs](scripts/verify-export.mjs)<br>[tests/data-exit-contract.mjs](tests/data-exit-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/data-exit-smoke.mjs](tests/data-exit-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6M-DATA-EXIT.md](docs/current/SLICE-6M-DATA-EXIT.md)<br>[lib/saha/export-audit.ts](lib/saha/export-audit.ts)<br>[tests/review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6P-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6P-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[lib/saha/audit-chain-server.ts](lib/saha/audit-chain-server.ts)<br>[lib/saha/operations-server.ts](lib/saha/operations-server.ts)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6Q-SECOND-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6Q-SECOND-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[lib/saha/pilot-server.ts](lib/saha/pilot-server.ts)<br>[tests/fourth-review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/fourth-review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6U-FOURTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6U-FOURTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[tests/sixth-review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/sixth-review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6Z-SIXTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6Z-SIXTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[app/data-exit-panel.tsx](app/data-exit-panel.tsx)<br>[scripts/reimport-export.mjs](scripts/reimport-export.mjs)<br>[tests/data-exit-portability-contract.mjs](tests/data-exit-portability-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SAHA-120-DATA-EXIT-LOCAL-CODING-COMPLETION.md](docs/current/SAHA-120-DATA-EXIT-LOCAL-CODING-COMPLETION.md) |

## BR-SEC

| Requirement | Priority / BRD phase | Assessment / target | Current behaviour | Remaining work | Related evidence context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BR-SEC-01 — Purpose and lawful authority | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Local AI/sharing flags and EHR purpose/direction/time permissions. | Approved processing inventory and enforceable authority/recipient/retention basis for every flow. | [lib/saha/server.ts](lib/saha/server.ts)<br>[lib/saha/access.ts](lib/saha/access.ts)<br>[lib/saha/store.ts](lib/saha/store.ts)<br>[REMAINING-RELEASES.md](REMAINING-RELEASES.md)<br>[lib/saha/consent.ts](lib/saha/consent.ts)<br>[app/consent-panel.tsx](app/consent-panel.tsx)<br>[tests/notice-consent-contract.mjs](tests/notice-consent-contract.mjs) |
| BR-SEC-02 — Understandable notice and consent | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Optional AI can be declined; contact preferences and EHR scopes are recorded. | Reviewed multilingual notices, notice versions, representative authority and full expiry semantics. | [lib/saha/server.ts](lib/saha/server.ts)<br>[lib/saha/access.ts](lib/saha/access.ts)<br>[lib/saha/store.ts](lib/saha/store.ts)<br>[REMAINING-RELEASES.md](REMAINING-RELEASES.md)<br>[lib/saha/consent.ts](lib/saha/consent.ts)<br>[app/consent-panel.tsx](app/consent-panel.tsx)<br>[tests/notice-consent-contract.mjs](tests/notice-consent-contract.mjs) |
| BR-SEC-03 — Withdrawal and downstream effects | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Revocation blocks pending use; contact changes cancel reminders; receipts remain visible. Slice 6o: withdrawing AI, sharing or contact permission (also with the switch) cancels queued and retryable deliveries, blocks unexecuted plans and prepared EHR publications, revokes EHR permissions and mock ABDM consents, stops unsent AI requests and pending reminders, and records each item as stopped, awaiting receipt, already sent or kept in a consent history and the audit chain. A06 adds original prepared-grant provenance, redacted own-only purge instructions after current care access is removed, encrypted revision-checked notebook/media deletion before durable client acknowledgement, and minimal tombstones preserving uncertain sync/media receipt references without blind care retries. Independent actual browser checks verify fail-closed known-refusal storage failures and offline reload on both surfaces; partial committed deletion batches survive later faults. Registration follow-up now records immutable original registration/match provenance, verifies the trusted original notebook account, clears cached candidate copies before connected reuse, and atomically removes known-withdrawn local registration copies while retaining unknown receipt proof. | BR-SEC-08 — Audit evidence | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Writes/approvals/exports have append-only audit and revisions. Slice 6j: per-organisation SHA-256 audit chain with genesis seal over earlier events, owner/administrator verification reporting the first break, and a JSONL auditor export (digest-only for out-of-scope patient events) recorded in the chain. Slice 6t: material reads (paged full patient record, revision history, visit media, received ABDM records, FHIR downloads) and external AI/EHR-retrieval requests each record one chained event (identifiers and counts, no clinical content; emergency-access route named) before content is returned or the request sent, shown in the Activity log. Owners/administrators can download a chain-head checkpoint (sequence, hash, digest; issuing it is chained) to keep outside Saha and later check it, which detects a suffix rewritten with recomputed hashes. Slice 6w: full records returned through the paged workspace window that the viewer has not opened through the detail route in the last 12 hours are recorded as record views first, and left out if that event cannot be written; a checkpoint past the 100,000-event verification limit is verified from an anchor (a checkpoint the owner names, or the last one recorded); indicator CSV exports are access-audit events. Slice 6aa: an unpaged workspace read by a non-ASHA viewer above the paging threshold records a viewPatientRecord event for every returned record not recorded in the last 12 hours before returning it (fail closed); the import rejection report is an access-audit event that moves no revision. Slice 10h: the remaining read paths record chained events before the read returns, refused if the event cannot be written: one summarised event per workspace snapshot load (scope size, records returned, paging window; also the snapshot returned with a command and with a replayed command), an explicit record-opened event reported by the workspace (the workspace shows the record only after the server answers; client-reported), patient searches, duplicate checks and patient or work-list pages (kind and result count, never the search text or entered details) and aggregate views (indicator results, the JSON operations and care report, aggregate report history; no cell values). Every access event now carries the acting membership's role, owner flag, membership version and the delegated authority in force inside its hashed detail; the default Activity log page leaves summary events out unless asked. Current app-first integration audits care continuity, referral escalations and action plans before return with count-only fixed labels. Distinct viewActionPlan avoids suppressing full-record access audits; independent actual 507-patient fault/recovery reproduction verifies this. Response metadata batching preserves existing SQL predicates and durable access ordering; independent SQL and Worker review passes. At the earlier audit checkpoint, offline opens, delta responses and external anchor lifecycle remained. Current command deltas append atomic count-only partition and per-record read events before return. Saved receipt replay now uses a fresh scoped/paged material load; per-record audit refusal omits patient data under the existing read-window contract without repeating the saved mutation. New common-persist command events append actor pre-mutation membership/version/owner and server-recorded delegation-use metadata inside their hashed detail; direct untracked uses are explicit, and prior history remains unchanged. Encrypted app/field material-open records now capture original grant provenance before plaintext display; metadata-only uploads require the trusted originating account before parsing/replay/writes, retain historical scope, and never renew care authority. Explicitly abandoned never-dispatched links retire only with current encrypted-book proof; unknown and accepted evidence stays retained. D02 isolated /today candidate reuses server scope and durable audit with a read-only identity-context projection; see docs/current/d02/SPECIFICATION.md and VALIDATION.md. Formal and representative-worker acceptance remain pending. Overnight access/recovery checkpoint (11 October 2026): final consistent Routine/Today access checks, bounded ACCESS append liveness, original-organisation dispatch and original-account exact-attempt recovery have scoped native/browser and final203-unit/API/restore evidence. See docs/current/d03/OVERNIGHT-AUTHORITY-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUTHORITY.json. Current-head CI, complete applicable physical/reference-device NFR and hosted A10 remain pending; wider captured-member helper and formal acceptance are not closed. Integrated audit qualification (11 October 2026): current-membership append/postcheck and native excess-count witness pass fresh current305 full205/10791 guarded205; same-runtime canonical166/14529 passes guarded166 with one inherited media skip and excluded old13 unit fixture declared. All380 prior guard records remain exact. See docs/current/d03/AUDIT-GUARD-QUALIFICATION-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-AUDIT-GUARD.json for exact source/setup limits and saved Git receipts. Complete real UI/reference NFR02, originalNFR5, final-head CI, separate final review and A10 remain pending; physical/clinical/formal acceptance is unchanged. Current NFR checkpoint (11 October 2026): authorization63/0, classifier11/0 and native source-drift10/0 pass; load12/3 fails with360/400 slots complete under unchanged minima/budgets. Fixed1000 realUI control finishes8000 records, Routine p953132.309ms exceeds3s; desktop surrogate is not physical/R1 acceptance. Navigation proposal failed22/1 and is withheld, original Today unchanged. See docs/current/d03/NFR-CHECKPOINT-20261011.md and EVIDENCE-20261011-NFR-CHECKPOINT.json. Current-head CI/A9/A10 and formal criteria remain open; no implementation status is promoted. | Remaining browser-local search/uncovered material reads, direct operational writer authority coverage, unknown delegation tracking, external anchor signing/publication/key lifecycle, independent reconstruction and measured cost. Durable offline opens have bounded synthetic software evidence; physical-device and complete audit acceptance remain pending. | Slice 13e: chain retries back off randomly, so a burst of audited reads on one organisation no longer exhausts its attempts in a local contention model (50 of 120 refused before, none after); not measured on hosted D1. App-first probe followup reserves the EHR metadata probe command receipt and chained audit before transport; configured-suspended initial/reconfigured EHR/MIS tests are diagnostic only, with current owner/service/configuration rechecks and clinical active-only restrictions preserved. | NFR-18 — Operational observability | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Redacted request IDs, metrics and manual incidents. Slice 6g: computed alerts for uncertain receipts, overdue work, missing provider usage, error rate and open incidents. Slice 6i: exception rows link to the affected patient and visit. Slice 6v: in-app delivery to the owning role with delivery times, and escalation records; evaluated on reads and organisation-wide writes, so detection time depends on traffic (no background timer). Slice 6y: a read-only trace per visit, programme submission or EHR publication (`GET /api/trace`, linked from the visit's Receipts tab and the exception queue) lists each step from the user command to the execution checkpoint, the provider or destination receipt or callback and the audit event, with command type, actor, receipt key, attempt numbers and task limits, ids, states and times only; execution steps carry a correlation reference from this slice on (older steps show as not linked); scoped like the visit, with redacted delivery items for owners and administrators outside patient scope. Slice 6z: in-app escalation is suppressed only when every owning member's own view shows the acknowledgement, and no escalation notification is left on an occurrence a concurrent evaluation closed. Slice 6aa: a trace for an item outside the viewer's patient scope says whether a receipt exists without returning it, and trace look-ups keep the newest events and report when a list was cut (trace 1.1.0). Slice 10b: unacknowledged alerts are also sent to the next recipient tier after an owner-set interval as simulated SMS or email messages (demonstration simulator in synthetic and training environments only), each with a receipt, an audit event and a recorded due time against sent time; the log shows when a message was late because nobody used Saha at that moment. Slice 10a: a scheduled job (Cron Trigger every five minutes in the hosted configuration) evaluates alerts independent of traffic and marks provider and destination requests past their time limit as needing review (an assistant request keeps its status and gains a review mark; a programme delivery, EHR publication or aggregate report in flight over ten minutes becomes uncertain) with one audit event each naming the scheduled job; it never calls a provider or destination and never resends or retries; the trace shows the mark. Verified in contract tests and a local scheduled-handler check, not on Cloudflare. | Detection within five minutes measured on the hosted Worker (the trigger has not run there; it needs the Workers Paid plan and does bounded work per run, so a large deployment is reached over several runs), delivery of real out-of-app messages (slice 10b is a demonstration simulator), a trace id carried to destinations and stored with request ids, and correlation for steps recorded before slice 6y. | | NFR-22 — Data portability and export integrity | Must / R1 | partial / R15 | Local full-database restore, limited structured exports and a documented organisation export with identifiers, relationships and version metadata; a 1000-person synthetic export re-imports in the contract suite with matching counts and an intact audit chain. Slice 6p: the manifest counts withheld entries inside organisation documents and is complete only when none is withheld; the offline verifier checks every personId inside those documents against the exported patients and that completeness claim. Slice 6q: export audit retries also re-check care scope and, for the operations CSV, reporting teams and delegation ids. Slice 6z: the manifest counts other members' alert notifications left out (otherMembersRows); they are not patient rows and do not affect completeness. SAHA-120 adds a reusable verifier-gated validation re-import command for one full archive or all source-bound staged parts, plus an archive-specific dictionary for every observed structured path with explicit unit/null and provenance. A current source-adapted inherited run re-imported 1000 synthetic persons and 55 tables with complete relationships and intact audit chain; see docs/current/SAHA-120-DATA-EXIT-LOCAL-CODING-COMPLETION.md. | Independently operated 1000-person re-import, representative staged capacity, buyer sign-off of the documented format, exact-source CI/review and formal acceptance. | [lib/saha/fhir-document.ts](lib/saha/fhir-document.ts)<br>[lib/saha/operations.ts](lib/saha/operations.ts)<br>[REMAINING-RELEASES.md](REMAINING-RELEASES.md)<br>[lib/saha/data-exit.ts](lib/saha/data-exit.ts)<br>[lib/saha/data-exit-server.ts](lib/saha/data-exit-server.ts)<br>[scripts/verify-export.mjs](scripts/verify-export.mjs)<br>[tests/data-exit-contract.mjs](tests/data-exit-contract.mjs)<br>[tests/data-exit-smoke.mjs](tests/data-exit-smoke.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6M-DATA-EXIT.md](docs/current/SLICE-6M-DATA-EXIT.md)<br>[lib/saha/export-audit.ts](lib/saha/export-audit.ts)<br>[tests/review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6P-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6P-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6Q-SECOND-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6Q-SECOND-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[tests/sixth-review-fixes-contract.mjs](tests/sixth-review-fixes-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SLICE-6Z-SIXTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md](docs/current/SLICE-6Z-SIXTH-REVIEW-FIXES.md)<br>[app/data-exit-panel.tsx](app/data-exit-panel.tsx)<br>[scripts/reimport-export.mjs](scripts/reimport-export.mjs)<br>[tests/data-exit-portability-contract.mjs](tests/data-exit-portability-contract.mjs)<br>[docs/current/SAHA-120-DATA-EXIT-LOCAL-CODING-COMPLETION.md](docs/current/SAHA-120-DATA-EXIT-LOCAL-CODING-COMPLETION.md) |

| NFR-24 — Scale readiness | Should / R2 | unverified / R16 | Slice 0a adds patient-partitioned loads and per-patient commit guards; slice 0b-2 scopes workspace reads to the viewer, so a local probe measured an ASHA read at 48 ms with 50,006 people (965 ms before). Slice 0b-3 pages non-ASHA scopes above 500 people: the same local probe measured a whole-team supervisor's paged workspace read at 34 ms and 5 KB, a 50-row list page at 38 ms and one patient's detail at 18 ms with 50,006 people, against 1.9 s and 38.6 MB for the unpaged read; search scans the scope (149 ms). Local machine only, not a reference device or hosted D1. Not scale evidence. Slice 6q: the patient-list cursor is opaque and name-free (`after:<id>`, resolved in the viewer's scope) and search text is sent in a POST body. Slice 0b-4: in a paged workspace, My work, follow-ups, the referral tracker and Submissions are built on the server over the whole scope (`GET /api/worklists`, one page per request, same order and counts as the unpaged workspace); on a shared local machine the work view took 557 ms and 11 KB at 20,006 people against 2.3 s and 18.1 MB for the full read, growing with the scope because each page orders the whole scope; the 50,006-person step stalled and was not measured. Slice 6w: work-list cursors carry the view's total as well as the offset; when the total has changed the server restarts at the first page and the panel replaces its rows instead of appending. Slice 13e: with audit indexes (migration 0017), 1,000 workspace loads took 23.8 s then 24.3 s over 4,000 loads (24.9 s then 32.1 s without them) in Miniflare; Worker memory growth is unchanged and remains open. Local macOS process-memory measurement now runs rather than skips: 8,101 synthetic workspace requests returned 200, but post-first mean growth remained 31.86 KiB/load; no plateau or hosted isolate-memory qualification is established. Matched local memory profiling completed 4,202 synthetic requests: all-isolate JS used heap returned from 106.8 to 16.6 MiB after snapshots, while RSS remained 340.2 MiB; reachable class counts were broadly stable. This narrows the local symptom toward collectable allocations/runtime cadence and does not establish a native allocation stack or close O-2. A bounded 2,000-request no-inspector control series narrows the symptom: minimal, transient-request and fixed read-only D1 cases grow less than the full Saha workspace; twenty seconds natural idle did not reclaim measured resident memory. The actual cause and a production fix remain open. | Partitioned architecture, topology/load/support/cost review before 10000-worker or five-million-person expansion. |
